===Original===
To the Gentlemen of the Saturday Club, this narrative of a short absence from home and from their society, is dedicated.

===Chinese===
谨以这篇记述短暂离家、暂别诸君的小文，献给星期六俱乐部（Saturday Club）的诸位先生。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. I have no right to introduce the public to the house of Mr. C﻿—﻿—. But that has already been done. Many tourists, and last and most unreservedly of all, Miss Bremer, in her *Homes of the New World*, have already given it such publicity, that I have thought my lighter step would not be felt on the beaten way. ↩︎
===Chinese===
1. 我无权把公众引进 C——先生的宅邸。但此事早已有人做了。许多游历者，而其中最后也最不保留的，是布雷默小姐（Miss Bremer）在她的《新世界的家园》（*Homes of the New World*）里——早已使它广为人知，因此我想，我这双更轻的脚步，在这条走熟的路上是不会被人觉出的。 ↩︎

===Original===
2. While these sheets are in press, the newspapers report that a fire has spread over a section of country between Matanzas and Cardenas, not only destroying the standing cane, but burning up houses, sugar mills, and the sugar and molasses stored for the market. Several lives were lost by the conflagration, which affected, more or less above twenty plantations. ↩︎
===Chinese===
2. 本书付印之际，报纸报道：马坦萨斯与卡德纳斯（Cardenas）之间的一片乡野失火蔓延，不仅烧毁了田里生长的甘蔗，还烧掉了房屋、糖坊，以及储存待售的糖和糖蜜。这场大火夺去了几条人命，波及的种植园不下二十座，轻重不一。 ↩︎

===Original===
3. Since my return, it has been officially announced that a commission is to be appointed to revise and reduce the tariffs of duties. ↩︎
===Chinese===
3. 我归来之后，已有官方宣布：将任命一个委员会，修订并降低关税。 ↩︎

## I Departure from New York﻿—Steamship *Cahawba*﻿—First day / 一　从纽约启程——「卡哈巴」号轮船——第一天

===Original===
Saturday, the twelfth day of February, 1859, is a dull, dark day in New York, with visitations of snow squalls, as the United States Mail Steamer *Cahawba* swings at her pier, at the foot of Robinson Street﻿—a pier crowded with drays and drivers, and a street of mud, snow and ice, and poor habitations. The steamer is to sail at one p.m.; and, by half-past twelve, her decks are full, and the mud and snow of the pier are well trodden by men and horses. Coaches drive down furiously, and nervous passengers put their heads out to see if the steamer is off before her time; and on the decks, and in the gangways, inexperienced passengers run against everybody, and mistake the engineer for the steward, and come up the same stairs they go down, without knowing it. In the dreary snow, the newspaper vendors cry the papers, and the book vendors thrust yellow covers into your face﻿—“Reading for the voyage, sir﻿—five hundred pages, close print!” And that being rejected, they reverse the process of the Sibyl﻿—with “Here’s another, sir, one thousand pages, double columns.” The great beam of the engine moves slowly up and down, and the black hull sways at its fasts. A motley group are the passengers. Shivering Cubans, exotics that have taken slight root in the hothouses of the Fifth Avenue, are to brave a few days of sleet and cold at sea, for the palm trees and mangoes, the cocoas and orange trees, they will be sitting under in six days, at farthest. There are Yankee shipmasters going out to join their “cotton wagons” at New Orleans and Mobile, merchants pursuing a commerce that knows no rest and no locality: confirmed invalids advised to go to Cuba to die under mosquito nets and be buried in a Potter’s Field; and other invalids wisely enough avoiding our March winds; and here and there a mere vacation-maker, like myself.

===Chinese===
1859 年 2 月 12 日，星期六，纽约是个阴沉晦暗的日子，阵阵暴风雪不时袭来。合众国邮轮「卡哈巴」号（*Cahawba*）泊在罗宾逊街尽头的码头上，随水轻晃——码头挤满了运货马车和车夫，街上泥泞、积雪、结着冰，两旁是破败的房舍。轮船定于下午一时启航；到十二点半，甲板上已挤满了人，码头上的泥雪被人马踩得稀烂。马车一辆接一辆疾驰而来，神经紧张的乘客探出头来，生怕轮船提前开走；甲板上、舷梯间，没有经验的新手东碰西撞，把轮机员当成乘务员，下楼又从同一道楼梯走上来而浑然不觉。凄迷的雪中，卖报的小贩叫喊着报纸，卖书的把黄色封皮直往你脸前递——「旅途的消遣读物，先生——五百页，小字密排！」被拒绝之后，他们便反其道而行，学起西比拉女巫的做派——「这本如何，先生，一千页，双栏排印。」发动机的巨梁缓缓上下移动，黑色的船身在系缆间摇荡。乘客们真是形形色色。瑟缩发抖的古巴人——这些在第五大道暖房里浅浅扎了根的异乡花草——要熬过几天海上的冻雨严寒，去换取最迟六天之后便能坐在其下的棕榈与芒果、可可与橘树。还有扬基船长们，赶往新奥尔良和莫比尔去与他们的「运棉车队」会合；有孜孜逐利、不知休止、不拘一地的商贾；有被劝去古巴等死的沉疴病人——好在蚊帐底下咽气，葬入贫民公墓；也有别的病客，倒是明智得很，来躲避我们这儿的三月朔风；再就是零零星星几个纯粹休假的人，比如我。

===Original===
Captain Bullock is sure to sail at the hour; and at the hour he is on the paddle box, the fasts are loosed, the warp run out, the crew pull in on the warp on the port quarter, and the head swings off. No word is spoken, but all is done by signs; or, if a word is necessary, a low clear tone carries it to the listener. There is no tearing and rending escape of steam, deafening and distracting all, and giving a kind of terror to a peaceful scene; but our ship swings off, gathers way, and enters upon her voyage, in a quiet like that of a bank or counting room, almost under a spell of silence.

The housetops and piers and hilltops are lined with snow, the masts and decks are white with it, a dreary cold haze lies over the water, and we work down the bay, where few sails venture out, and but few are coming in; and only a strong monster of a Cunard screw-steamer, the *Kangaroo*, comes down by our side.

===Chinese===
布洛克船长（Captain Bullock）必定准时启航；到时候他站在桨轮罩上，系缆解开，倒缆放出，船员在左舷后部收缆，船头缓缓摆开。无人发一言，全凭手势行事；真需要说话时，也只低沉而清晰的一声，便送到听者耳中。没有蒸汽撕裂般的呼啸——那种震耳欲聋、令人心神俱乱、给平和景象平添几分恐怖的声响；我们的船悄然而动，渐生航速，踏上旅程，那份安静就像银行里或账房中一般，几乎像中了沉默的咒。

屋顶、码头、山巅都镶着雪边，桅杆和甲板覆雪皆白，凄冷的雾霭笼罩水面。我们的船顺湾而下，湾中少有帆船敢出航，进港的也不多；只有一艘冠达公司的螺旋桨巨轮「袋鼠」号（*Kangaroo*），跟着我们旁边驶下来。

===Original===
We leave city and suburbs Brooklyn Heights, and the foggy outline of Staten Island, far behind us, and hurry through the Narrows, for the open sea. The *Kangaroo* crossed our hawse in a strange way. Is she steering wild, or what is it? Seeing two old unmistakable Yankee shipmasters, sitting confidentially together on two chairs, in affectionate proximity to the binnacle, I address myself to them, and my question, being put in proper nautical phrase, secures a respectful attention. I find they agree with me that the *Kangaroo* is a little wilful, and crosses our hawse on purpose, in some manoeuvre to discharge her pilot before we do ours; and so thinks the quartermaster, who comes aft to right the colors. This manoeuvering of the steamer and pilot vessel makes an incident for a few minutes’ talk, and an opening for several acquaintances which will be voyage-long. The pilots are dropped into their little cockboats, and their boats drop astern, and go bobbing over the seas, to the pilot schooner that lies to for them. The *Kangaroo*, with her mysterious submarine art of swimming without fins, stands due east for Liverpool, and we stand down the coast, southerly, for the regions of the Sun.

===Chinese===
我们把城市和郊野、布鲁克林高地，连同斯塔滕岛雾蒙蒙的轮廓，远远抛在身后，急急穿过纳罗斯水道，驶向大海。「袋鼠」号以一种古怪的方式横越我们的船头。是她操舵失当，还是别的什么缘故？我看见两位一望便知是老扬基船长的人，亲密地并肩坐在罗经柜近旁的两把椅子上，便凑上前去搭话；我的问题用了地道的航海措辞，赢得了他们恭敬的注意。我发现他们与我所见相同：「袋鼠」号有点任性，是故意横越我们的船头，耍个花招，好抢在我们之前送走她的领航员；来船尾整理旗号的舵手也这么想。轮船与领航艇的这一番机动，成了几分钟的谈资，也开了头，引出几段将延续整个旅程的交情。领航员们被放下他们的小划子，小划子落在船尾之后，在浪头上颠簸着驶向为他们候命的领航帆船。「袋鼠」号以她那不用鳍便能游水的神秘水下技艺，取正东航向驶往利物浦；我们则沿海南下，驶向太阳的国度。

===Original===
The Heights of Neversink are passed. The night closes in upon the sea, dreary, cold, and snowing; our signal lanterns, the red, the white, and the green, gleam out into the mist; the furnace fires throw a lurid light from the doors below, cheerful or fearful as may be the temper of mind of the looker-on; the long swell lifts and drops the bow and stern, and rolls the ship from side to side; the sea-bells begin to strike their strange reckoning of the half-hours; the wet and the darkness drive all below but the experts and the desperate, and our first night at sea has begun.

At six bells, tea is announced; and the bright lights of the long cabin table, shining on plates and cups and gleaming knives and hurrying waiters, make a cheerful and lively contrast with the dark, cold, deserted deck.

===Chinese===
尼弗辛克高地驶过去了。夜幕向海上合拢，阴冷，飘着雪；我们的信号灯——红的、白的、绿的——在雾中闪烁；炉火从下面炉门里投出幽红的光，在旁观者眼中是温暖还是可怖，全看他当时的心境；长长的涌浪把船头船尾抬起又放下，船身左右横滚；船钟开始敲响它们每半小时一次的奇妙计时报点；湿冷与黑暗把所有人都赶进舱里，只有老手和亡命之徒还留在上面，我们海上生活的第一夜开始了。

六击钟时，宣布用茶；长餐桌上的明亮灯光，照着盘盏杯壶、闪亮的刀叉和往来穿梭的侍者，与漆黑冰冷、寂无人迹的甲板形成欢快而生动的对照。

===Original===
By night, I walk deck for a couple of hours with the young captain. After due inquiries about his family in Georgia, and due remembrance of those of his mother’s line whom we loved, and the public honored, before the grave or the sea closed over them, the fascinating topic of the navy, the frigates and the line-of-battle ships and little sloops, the storms, the wrecks, and the sea fights, fill up the time. He loves the navy still, and has left it with regret; but the navy does not love her sons as they love her. On the quarterdeck at fifteen, the first in rank of his year, favored by his commanders, with service in the best vessels, making the great fleet cruise under Morris, taking part in the actions of the Naval Brigade on shore in California, serving on the Coast Survey, a man of science as well as a sailor﻿—yet what is there before him, or those like him, in our navy? The best must continue a subaltern, a lieutenant, until he is gray. At fifty, he may be entitled to his first command, and that of a class below a frigate; and if he survives the African fevers and the Isthmus fevers, and the perils of the sea, he may totter on the quarterdeck of a line-of-battle ship when his skill is out of date and his capacity for further command problematical. And whatever may be the gallantry or the merit of his service, though he may cut off his right hand or pluck out his eye for the country’s honor, the navy can give him no promotion, not even a barren title of brevet, nor a badge of recognition of merit, though it be but a star, or a half yard of blue ribbon. The most meritorious officers receive large offers from civil life; and then, it is home, family, society, education of children, and pecuniary competency on the one side, and on the other, only the navy, less and less attractive as middle life draws on.

===Chinese===
入夜后，我同年轻的船长在甲板上散步一两个小时。先照例问过他佐治亚家中亲人的情形，又照例怀念了他母系中那些我们敬爱、公众尊崇、而今已入坟墓或葬身大海的亲人；随后，那个令人着迷的话题——海军，快速帆舰（frigate）、战列舰和小炮舰，风暴、海难与海战——便填满了这段时光。他至今热爱海军，离开时满怀怅惘；可是海军爱她的儿子们，远不如儿子们爱她。他十五岁便登上后甲板，是同届中的第一名，深受长官垂青，在最好的舰船上服役，随莫里斯统率的庞大舰队巡航，在加利福尼亚参加过海军旅的陆上战斗，在海岸测量局（Coast Survey）任过职，既是学者又是水手——然而在我们的海军里，他或与他相似的人，前程又在哪里呢？最优秀的人也得当初级军官、当海军上尉，一直熬到两鬓斑白。到五十岁，他也许才轮得到第一次独当一面的指挥权，还是比快速帆舰低一等的舰种；倘若他熬过了非洲的热病和地峡的热病，躲过了海上的种种危难，等他终于颤巍巍登上战列舰的后甲板时，技艺早已过时，还能否再执指挥之权也成了疑问。而且无论他的服役多么英勇、功绩多么卓著，哪怕他为国家体面砍去右手、剜出眼睛，海军也无法给他晋升，连一个空头的荣誉军衔都给不了，连一枚表彰功绩的徽章也没有——哪怕只是一颗星，或者半码蓝绶带。功勋最著的军官们会接到民间生活的优厚邀约；于是一边是家庭、天伦、社交、儿女的教育和宽裕的进项，另一边却只剩海军，人到中年，它的吸引力正一天天减退。

===Original===
The staterooms of the *Cahawba*, like those of most American seagoing steamers, are built so high above the water that the windows may be open in all but the worst of weather, and good ventilation be ensured. I have a very nice fellow for my roommate, in the berth under me; but, in a stateroom, no roommate is better than the best; so I change my quarters to a stateroom further forward, nearer “the eyes of her,” which the passengers generally shun, and get one to myself, free from the rattle of the steering gear, while the delightful rise and fall of the bows, and leisurely weather roll and lee roll, cradle and nurse one to sleep.

===Chinese===
「卡哈巴」号的舱房，同多数美国远洋轮船的一样，建在离水面很高的地方，除了最坏的天气都可以开窗，保证良好的通风。同舱的是位很不错的小伙子，睡我下铺；不过在舱房里，再好的室友也不如没有室友；于是我挪到更靠前的一间舱房，离「她的眼睛」更近——乘客们大都躲着这个位置——独自占用一间，听不见操舵装置的咔嗒声，而船头那令人心怡的起落，和从容不迫的向风横摇、背风横摇，像摇篮与乳母一般把人哄入梦乡。

## II Hatteras﻿—Gulf Stream﻿—Coast of Florida﻿—Routine of steamer / 二　哈特拉斯——湾流——佛罗里达海岸——轮船的日常

===Original===
*Sunday, February 13*.﻿—It is cold and rough, though not at all stormy, and those who are on deck wear thick coats and caps. There is no clergyman on board, and we have no religious service. Capt. Bullock used to read the Liturgy himself, but in these West India and New Orleans voyages there are many Roman Catholics, and those who are not Romanists are of so many denominations, that he received little encouragement in maintaining an official worship; and it is no longer held, unless there is a clergyman on board and a request is made by the passengers.

All day there has been no sail in sight, except the steamer *Columbia*, for Charleston, SC; and she soon disappeared below the horizon.

===Chinese===
*2 月 13 日，星期日*。——天气寒冷，风浪不小，虽全然算不上风暴，在甲板上的人都穿起厚外套、戴起帽子。船上没有牧师，我们也就没有礼拜。布洛克船长从前亲自诵读公祷文；但在这些西印度和新奥尔良航线上有许多罗马天主教徒，而非天主教徒又分属如此之多的教派，他坚持举行正式礼拜几乎得不到鼓励；于是礼拜不再举行，除非船上有牧师，且由乘客提出请求。

一整天不见一张帆影，只有开往南卡罗来纳州查尔斯顿的「哥伦比亚」号（*Columbia*）轮船例外，不久它也消失在地平线之下了。

===Original===
We are near Cape Hatteras. It is night, and soon the Light of Hatteras throws its bright, cheerful beam for thirty miles over a huge burial ground of sailors. How many struggles with death, how many last efforts of the last resources of skill and courage, what floating wrecks of ships, what waste of life, has that light shone over! Under that reef, perished Bache, flying for harbor before the gale, in his little surveying brig. Every league has been and will be a field where lives and treasures are sown thick from the hand of Destruction﻿—one of those points on the earth’s surface where, in the universal and endless struggle between life and death, preservation and destruction, the destroyers have the advantage.

Soon after 9 p.m. we stand out direct, to cross the Gulf Stream. A bucket is thrown over the side, and water drawn. Its temperature is at 42°. In fifteen minutes more, it is thrown again, and the water is at 72° 30′. We are in the Gulf Stream.

===Chinese===
我们正在哈特拉斯角附近。夜色中，哈特拉斯灯塔很快便把它明亮欢畅的光柱投向三十英里外，照着一片水手的巨大坟场。多少与死亡的搏斗，多少技能与勇气在最后关头的最后努力，多少漂流的船骸，多少生命的虚掷，都在那灯光下上演过！在那道礁石旁，贝奇乘着他的小小测量双桅船，抢在风暴前夺港避风，终至覆没。这里的每一里格都曾经是、也将继续是毁灭之手密密播撒生命与财宝的田地——这是地球表面上那样的一些地点之一：在生与死、保存与毁灭那普遍而无尽的争斗中，毁灭者占据着上风。

晚九点过后不久，我们径直向外驶去，横渡湾流（Gulf Stream）。一只桶从舷边抛下，打上水来。水温 42 度。一刻钟后再抛一次，水已是 72 度 30 分。我们已置身湾流之中。

===Original===
*Monday, February 14*.﻿—Sea rather rough, and a good deal of seasickness. Several passengers have not been seen since we left the dock, and only about half appear at table. We are to the eastward of the Gulf Stream. The weather is clear, and no longer cold. At noon, we are in about the latitude of Charleston, SC. No vessels in sight, all day. It is strange, and always excites the surprise and comment of seafaring men, that in the great highway of nations, with the immense commerce that is perpetually running East and West, North and South, a steamer may make her three hundred miles a day, for day after day, and see no sails.

This is a truly glorious moonlight night. The seas and floods “in wavering morrice move”; the air is pure and not cold, the sky a deep blue, the sea a deep blue, the stars glisten, and the moon bathes all in a serene glory. It is hard to leave the deck and such a scene, for the small stateroom and its sleeping-shelf. But there must be sleep for infirm human nature﻿—a nature that has even less self-sustaining power than a locomotive engine, and must not only be supplied with fuel and water at every stopping place, but must lie by, in a dark corner, in absolute repose and mere oblivion, for one quarter of its time, or it will wear out in a few days.

===Chinese===
*2 月 14 日，星期一*。——海上风浪颇大，晕船的人不少。几位乘客自离码头后就没再露面，到餐桌前露面的只有大约一半人。我们在湾流东侧。天气晴朗，不再寒冷。正午时，我们约在南卡罗来纳州查尔斯顿的纬度上。一整天不见船影。真是奇怪，也总是引得航海人惊讶议论：在这条万国往来通衢的大道上，在东西南北永不停歇的巨量贸易之中，一艘轮船一天跑上三百英里，日复一日，竟可以不见一张帆。

这真是个无比壮丽的月夜。洪波巨浪「如跳莫里斯舞般摇曳起伏」（弥尔顿语）；空气纯净而不冷，天空是深蓝，海是深蓝，星光闪烁，月亮把一切沐浴在一片安详的荣光里。要离开甲板、离开这样的景色，回到小小的舱房和它的睡架上去，实在难以割舍。可是孱弱的人性必须有睡眠——这种本性连自我维持的力量都不如一台机车，不仅每到一处停靠都要添煤加水，还必须在一处黑暗的角落里停下来，处于绝对的静息与纯粹的忘却之中，占去它四分之一的时间，否则不出几天就要耗损殆尽。

===Original===
*Tuesday, February 15*.﻿—A bright, sunny cheerful day. Passengers have laid aside their thick coats and fur caps, the snow and ice are gone from the rigging and spars, the decks are dry, the sea is calm, and the steady going engine alone, with easy exercise of power, drives the great hull, with its freight of cargo and provisions and human beings, over the placid sea, as fast as a furious gale could drive it, and leaves her long wake of foam on the sea, and her long wake of dark smoke in the sky.

The passengers are recovering from seasickness. The women sit on deck and sew and read, and the children play. That family of Creole children﻿—how sallow, how frail, what delicate limbs, yet not without life, and with no little grace! But they are petted, and the girls complain, and the boys are disposed to tyrannize over the other boys and the dogs. It is interesting to see, or to fancy we see the effect not only of climate, but of slavery and of despotic institutions, on the characters of children. What career is there for Cuban youth of ambition or merit? and what must be their life without one?

===Chinese===
*2 月 15 日，星期二*。——晴朗明媚、令人畅快的一天。乘客们脱下了厚外套和皮帽，索具和桅桁上的积雪残冰不见了，甲板干燥，海面平静，唯有那台匀速运转的发动机，从容施展着力量，推动巨大的船身，连同它装载的货物、给养和人群，驶过平静的海面，速度之快不亚于一阵狂风的驱迫，在海上留下长长的泡沫尾迹，在天上留下长长的黑烟尾迹。

乘客们正从晕船中恢复过来。女人们坐在甲板上做针线、读书，孩子们玩耍。那一家的克里奥尔（Creole）孩子——多么苍白，多么羸弱，四肢多么纤秀，却并非没有生气，而且不乏风致！可是他们被宠坏了，女孩们抱怨个不停，男孩们则要对别的男孩和狗们作威作福。看着气候的影响、乃至奴役制度和专制制度对儿童性情的影响——或者只是自以为看到了——是很叫人玩味的事。有抱负、有才干的古巴青年能有什么前程？而没有前程，他们的生活又当如何？

===Original===
I am feeling very much at home in the *Cahawba*. She is an excellent sea boat, and under the best of discipline. I hardly believed that her commander could﻿—that any commander could﻿—fully come up to all the praise that had been bestowed on him; but I think he weathers it all. The rule of quietness prevails, almost to the point of an English dinner party. No order is given unless it be necessary, and none louder than is necessary for it to be heard. The reports are made in low voices, and the passengers are to see and hear as little as possible of the discipline of the ship. They do not know the quiet but certain means for ensuring the performance of every duty. They do not know that reports are made of the state of every part of the ship, and that, through the night, the cabins and passageways and every place where fire can take, are watched, and that the watch reports every half hour. They have not learned the merits of sturdy, faithful Miller, the chief mate, or quick, plucky Porter, the second mate, who can hardly keep down his “Liner” training to the tone of the Mail Steamer, nor the thorough excellence of the Engineer. But they do know the capital qualities of Mr. Rodgers, the purser, a grandson of the old Commodore, a nephew of Perry, and connected by blood or marriage with half the navy﻿—for his station and duties are among the passengers, and all become his personal friends.

The routine of the ship, as regards passengers, is this: a cup of coffee, if you desire it, when you turn out; breakfast at eight, lunch at twelve, dinner at three, tea at seven, and lights put out at ten.

===Chinese===
我在「卡哈巴」号上觉得宾至如归。她是条出色的海船，纪律也是最好的一等。我原先不大相信她的船长——不相信任何船长——能完全当得起人们给他的全部赞誉；但我想他全都经受得住。安静的规矩在这里通行无阻，几乎赶得上英国人的晚宴。没有必要的命令绝不下达，下达了也只以听得见的最低声调。报告都是低声作的，乘客对船上的纪律要尽量少看少听。他们不知道那些确保每项职责得以履行的、无声而确实的手段。他们不知道船上每个部位的状况都有人报告，整夜里，舱房、通道和每一处可能起火的地方都有人守望，值更者每半小时报告一次。他们无从知晓坚定忠实的大副米勒的好处、敏捷果敢的二副波特的好处——他那一身「战列舰」（Liner）的做派一时还压不到邮轮的调门上来——也无从知晓轮机长那份彻底的干练。但罗杰斯先生（Mr. Rodgers）的好处他们是知道的，这位事务长是老准将的孙子、佩里的侄子，与半个海军沾亲带故——因为他的岗位和职责正在乘客中间，人人都成了他的私交。

就乘客而言，船上的日常是这样的：起床时若愿意，来一杯咖啡；八点早饭，十二点午餐，三点正餐，七点茶点，十点熄灯。

===Original===
*Wednesday, February 16*.﻿—Beautiful, serene, summer sea! The thermometer is at 70°, awnings are spread, the ladies have their books and sewing on deck, the men read and play chess and smoke, and the children play. We have crossed the Gulf Stream again, and are skirting along the Coast of Florida, as near to shore as safety permits; and here the deep sea runs close to the land. All objects on shore are plainly discernible by the naked eye, from the deck. We are below St. Augustine, about halfway between that and Key West. The coast is an interminable reach of sand beach, with coral reefs before it and the Everglades behind it. There are three small white tents, on the green sward, close upon the beach, backed by a grove of trees, with signals flying. That is the station of the United States Coast Survey. Towards evening, we pass a rough camp which was one of the camps of “Billy Bowlegs,” the famous Seminole warrior. There is the wreck of a bark, her lower masts still standing, while the beach is strewn with casks and boxes. It is an old wreck, and they make no signal for aid.

After dark, a light is made on our starboard bow. It is Cape Florida Light. At 11 p.m. we make the light on Carysfort Reef, the outermost and southernmost of the Florida lights; and, having given a good berth to the reef, stand out to sea again, to cross the Gulf Stream the third time.

===Chinese===
*2 月 16 日，星期三*。——美丽、宁静的夏日之海！温度计 70 度，遮阳篷张开了，女士们在甲板上读书做针线，男人们读书、下棋、抽烟，孩子们玩耍。我们再次渡过湾流，正沿着佛罗里达海岸，在安全允许的范围内尽量贴近岸边行驶；在这里，深海紧逼着陆地。岸上的一切景物，从甲板上用肉眼便看得分明。我们已在圣奥古斯丁以南，约在它与基韦斯特之间的中点。海岸是一望无际的连绵沙滩，前面是珊瑚礁，后面是大沼泽地（Everglades）。紧贴沙滩的绿草地上支着三顶白色小帐篷，背后是一丛树林，信号旗飘扬着。那是合众国海岸测量局的测站。傍晚时分，我们经过一处粗陋的营地，那是「比利·鲍莱格斯」（Billy Bowlegs）——著名的塞米诺尔战士——的一处营盘。那里有一艘三桅帆船的残骸，下桅还立着，滩头散落着桶子和箱子。那是艘年久的残骸了，他们没有发出求救的信号。

天黑之后，右船首方向亮起一盏灯。那是佛罗里达角灯塔。晚十一时，我们望见卡里斯福特礁上的灯，那是佛罗里达各灯塔中最外、最南的一座；给那礁石让出足够的距离之后，我们重新向外海驶去，第三次横渡湾流。

===Original===
What can exceed the beauty of these nights at sea﻿—these moonlight nights, the still sea, those bright stars, the light, soft trade wind clouds floating under them, the gentle air, and a feeling of tropical romance stealing over the exile from the snow and ice of New England! There is something in the clear blue warm sea of the tropics, which gives to the stranger a feeling of unreality. Where do those vessels come from, that rise out of the sea, in the horizon? Where do they go to, as they sink in the sea again? Are those blue spots really fast anchored islands, with men and children, and horses, and machinery, and schools, politics and newspapers on them, or are they afloat, and visited by beings of the air?

===Chinese===
还有什么能胜过这些海上之夜的美——这些月夜，寂静的海，那些明亮的星，星下飘浮的轻软信风云，和煦的微风，以及一种热带的浪漫情愫，悄然袭上这个从新英格兰冰雪中放逐出来的人！热带清澈湛蓝的暖海里自有一种东西，给异乡人一种不真实之感。那些从海平线上跃出波面的船，是从哪里来的？它们再沉入海中去时，又往哪里去？那些蓝点子真是下锚定泊的岛屿，上面住着男人和孩子、马匹和机器，有学校、政治和报纸吗？还是它们只是漂在水上，受着空中生灵的造访？

## III The passengers﻿—Warm weather﻿—Coast of Cuba﻿—Pan of Matanzas﻿—First view of Havana, from the sea﻿—Night off Havana / 三　乘客——暖天气——古巴海岸——马坦萨斯面包山——海上初望哈瓦那——哈瓦那海外之夜

===Original===
*Thursday, February 17*.﻿—Again a beautiful, warm day. I wake, and the first glance out of my stateroom window shows the sea and sky flushed with the red of a bright sunrise. Awnings are spread; straw-hats and linen coats are worn; sewing, reading, and chess-playing is going on among the elders and the children are romping about the decks, beginning to feel entirely at home. There are boys from the Northern States, with fair skins and light hair, strong, loud-voiced, plainly dressed, in stout shoes, honest and awkward; and there are Cuban boys, with a mixed air of the passionate and the timorous, sallow, slender, small-voiced, graceful, but with the grace rather of girls than of boys, wearing slippers, ornamented waistcoats and jackets and hats with broad bands of cord. What preternaturally black eyes those little Creole girls have! Are they really eyes, so out of proportion in size and effect to their small thin faces? Their mother is hale and full fleshed, and probably they will come to the same favour at last.

===Chinese===
*2 月 17 日，星期四*。——又是一个美丽温暖的日子。我一觉醒来，从舱房窗口望出去的第一眼，便看见海与天都被灿烂的朝阳染红了。遮阳篷张着；人们戴上草帽、穿上亚麻外套；大人们做着针线、读着书、下着棋，孩子们在甲板上嬉闹，开始觉得全然无拘无束。有北方各州来的男孩，皮肤白皙，头发浅色，身子结实，嗓门洪亮，衣着朴素，脚上是结实的鞋，一脸诚实而笨拙的神气；也有古巴男孩，神气里混杂着热烈与怯懦，面色苍黄，身形纤细，声音细小，姿态优雅——却是少女式的优雅而非男孩的优雅——脚上趿着拖鞋，穿着缀饰的背心、短上衣，帽子缀着宽宽的绳带。那些克里奥尔小女孩的眼睛黑得出奇！那真是眼睛吗？与她们瘦小的脸庞相比，那尺寸和神采也太不相称了。她们的母亲健旺丰腴，她们最终大概也会长到同样丰润的模样。

===Original===
Throughout the day, sailing down the outer edge of the Gulf Stream, we see vessels of all forms and sizes, coming in sight and passing away, as in a dioramic show. There is a heavy cotton droger from the Gulf, of 1200 tons burden, under a cloud of sail, pressing on to the northern seas of New England or Old England. Here comes a saucy little Baltimore brig, close-hauled and leaning over to it; and there, half down in the horizon, is a pile of white canvas, which the experienced eyes of my two friends, the passenger shipmasters, pronounce to be a bark, outward bound. Every passenger says to every other, how beautiful! how exquisite! That pale thin girl who is going to Cuba for her health, her brother travelling with her, sits on the settee, propped by a pillow, and tries to smile and to think she feels stronger in this air. She says she shall stay in Cuba until she gets well!

===Chinese===
一整天，我们沿着湾流外缘南驶，看见大大小小、形形色色的船只，一一入眼而来，又一一逝去，宛如一场西洋景。有一艘来自墨西哥湾的重型运棉货船，载重一千二百吨，张着满帆，直奔新英格兰或老英格兰的北方海域而去。这边来了一艘俏丽的巴尔的摩双桅横帆船，紧帆迎风，船身倾侧；那边，半沉入地平线的地方，堆着一垛白帆，我那两位船主朋友的老练眼睛断定，那是一艘外航的三桅帆船。每个乘客都对别的乘客赞叹：多美啊！多妙啊！那个为养病前往古巴的苍白瘦弱的姑娘，她兄弟陪她同行，坐在长椅上，靠垫支撑着，勉强笑着，想让自己相信在这空气里觉得有力了些。她说她要留在古巴，一直住到病好！

===Original===
After dinner, Capt. Bullock tells us that we shall soon see the high lands of Cuba, off Matanzas; the first and highest being the Pan of Matanzas. It is clear over head, but a mist lies along the southern horizon, in the latter part of the day. The sharpest eyes detect the land, about 4 p.m., and soon it is visible to all. It is an undulating country on the coast, with high hills and mountains in the interior, and has a rich and fertile look. That height is the Pan, though we see no special resemblance, in its outline, to a loaf of bread. We are still sixty miles from Havana. We cannot reach it before dark, and no vessels are allowed to pass the Morro after the signals are dropped at sunset.

===Chinese===
正餐之后，布洛克船长告诉我们，很快就要望见马坦萨斯外海的古巴高地了；最先出现、也最高的一座，是马坦萨斯面包山（Pan of Matanzas）。头顶天色晴朗，但临近傍晚，南方地平线上横着一带雾霭。眼力最尖的人在下午四点左右辨认出了陆地，不久人人都看见了。那是一片沿海起伏的原野，内陆则是高冈和群山，看上去丰饶而膏腴。那处高地就是面包山，虽然我们在它的轮廓上看不出什么特别像一条面包的地方。我们离哈瓦那还有六十英里，天黑前赶不到了；而日落信号落下之后，任何船只都不许从莫罗前面通过。

===Original===
We coast the northern shore of Cuba, from Matanzas westward. There is no waste of sand and low flats, as in most of our southern states; but the fertile, undulating land comes to the sea, and rises into high hills as it recedes. “There is the Morro! and right ahead!” “Why, there is the city too! Is the city on the sea? We thought it was on a harbor or bay.” There, indeed, is the Morro, a stately hill of tawny rock, rising perpendicularly from the sea, and jutting into it, with walls and parapets and towers on its top, and flags and signals flying, and the tall lighthouse just in front of its outer wall. It is not very high, yet commands the sea about it. And there is the city, on the sea coast, indeed﻿—the houses running down to the coral edge of the ocean. Where is the harbor, and where the shipping? Ah, there they are! We open an entrance, narrow and deep, between the beetling Morro and the Punta; and through the entrance, we see the spreading harbor and the innumerable masts. But the darkness is gathering, the sunset gun has been fired, we can just catch the dying notes of trumpets from the fortifications, and the Morro Lighthouse throws its gleam over the still sea. The little lights emerge and twinkle from the city. We are too late to enter the port, and slowly and reluctantly the ship turns her head off to seaward. The engine breathes heavily, and throws its one arm leisurely up and down; we rise and fall on the moonlit sea; the stars are near to us, or we are raised nearer to them; the Southern Cross is just above the horizon; and all night long, two streams of light lie upon the water, one of gold from the Morro, and one of silver from the moon. It is enchantment. Who can regret our delay, or wish to exchange this scene for the common, close anchorage of a harbor?

===Chinese===
我们贴着古巴北岸，从马坦萨斯向西行驶。这里没有像我们南方各州大部分海岸那样的荒沙和低洼滩涂；丰饶起伏的陆地直抵海岸，向内陆退去时隆起为高冈。「莫罗在那儿！就在正前方！」「哎呀，城市也在那儿！城市就在海边吗？我们还以为它坐落在什么海港或海湾上呢。」那里确实是莫罗，一座巍峨的黄褐色岩石山，从海中垂直拔起，突出海中，顶上有城墙、雉堞和塔楼，旗号飘扬，高高的灯塔正立在外墙前面。它不算很高，却俯临着四周的海面。城市也确实就在海岸上——房屋一直延伸到海洋的珊瑚边缘。海港在哪里？船只又在哪里？啊，在那儿！我们望见一道入口，又窄又深，夹在峭拔的莫罗与蓬塔之间；穿过入口，便看见铺展开的海港和数不清的桅樯。然而暮色正在四合，日落炮已经放过，我们恰好还能捕捉到要塞里渐渐消逝的号音，莫罗灯塔把光华洒在平静的海上。城市的小小灯火次第亮起，闪烁着。我们进港已太迟，船头缓缓地、恋恋不舍地转向外海。发动机沉重地喘息，独臂悠闲地上下挥动；我们在月光照亮的海面上起伏；星星离我们近了，或者说我们被抬得离它们更近了；南十字星正悬在地平线上方；整整一夜，两道光带铺在水面上，一道金色，来自莫罗，一道银色，来自月亮。这真是令人着魔的仙境。谁会为我们的耽搁惋惜，或者想拿这景象去换海港里那寻常而逼仄的锚地呢？

## IV Enter Havana, at sunrise﻿—Harbor﻿—Shipping﻿—Landing﻿—Drive through streets of Havana﻿—Hotel / 四　日出进哈瓦那——海港——船舶——登岸——驱车穿行哈瓦那街巷——旅馆

===Original===
*Friday, February 18*.﻿—We are to go in at sunrise, and few, if any, are the passengers that are not on deck at the first glow of dawn. Before us lie the novel and exciting objects of the night before. The steep Morro, with its tall sentinel lighthouse, and its towers and signal staffs and teeth of guns, is coming out into clear daylight; the red and yellow striped flag of Spain﻿—blood and gold﻿—floats over it. Point after point in the city becomes visible; the blue and white and yellow houses, with their roofs of dull red tiles, the quaint old Cathedral towers, and the almost endless lines of fortifications. The masts of the immense shipping rise over the headland, the signal for leave to enter is run up, and we steer in under full head, the morning gun thundering from the Morro, the trumpets braying and drums beating from all the fortifications, the Morro, the Punta, the long Cabaña, the Casa Blanca and the city walls, while the broad sun is fast rising over this magnificent spectacle.

===Chinese===
*2 月 18 日，星期五*。——我们要在日出时分进港，晨光初现时尚未登上甲板的乘客，即使有也寥寥无几。昨夜那些新奇而动人的景物就在我们眼前。陡峭的莫罗，带着它高高的哨塔似的灯塔、塔楼、信号杆和牙齿般的一门门大炮，正显露在清晰的白昼里；西班牙那红黄条纹的旗帜——血与金——在它上空飘扬。城中一处接一处显露出来：蓝、白、黄的房屋，覆着暗红瓦的屋顶，古雅的老教堂钟塔，还有几乎望不到头的层层要塞工事。庞大船队的桅樯从海角后面升起，获准进港的信号升上桅顶，我们全速驶入，晨炮从莫罗轰鸣，号角齐鸣、鼓声震响，从所有的工事——莫罗、蓬塔、长长的卡瓦尼亚、白屋和城墙——一齐传来，而宽阔的太阳正冉冉升起，照临这一壮丽的奇观。

===Original===
What a world of shipping! The masts make a belt of dense forest along the edge of the city, all the ships lying head in to the street, like horses at their mangers; while the vessels at anchor nearly choke up the passageways to the deeper bays beyond. There are the red and yellow stripes of decayed Spain; the blue, white and red﻿—blood to the fingers’ end﻿—of La Grande Nation; the Union crosses of the Royal Commonwealth; the stars and stripes of the Great Republic, and a few flags of Holland and Portugal, of the states of Northern Italy, of Brazil, and of the republics of the Spanish Main. We thread our slow and careful way among these, pass under the broadside of a ship-of-the-line, and under the stern of a screw frigate, both bearing the Spanish flag, and cast our anchor in the Regla Bay, by the side of the steamer *Karnac*, which sailed from New York a few days before us. Instantly we are besieged by boats, some loaded with oranges and bananas, and others coming for passengers and their luggage, all with awnings spread over their sterns, rowed by sallow, attenuated men, in blue and white checks and straw hats, with here and there the familiar lips and teeth, and vacant, easily pleased face of the Negro. Among these boats comes one, from the stern of which floats the red and yellow flag with the crown in its field, and under whose awning reclines a man in a full suit of white linen, with straw hat and red cockade and a cigar. This is the Health Officer. Until he is satisfied, no one can come on board, or leave the vessel. Capt. Bullock salutes, steps down the ladder to the boat, hands his papers, reports all well﻿—and we are pronounced safe. Then comes another boat of similar style, another man reclining under the awning with a cigar, who comes on board, is closeted with the purser, compares the passenger list with the passports, and we are declared fully passed, and general leave is given to land with our luggage at the customhouse wharf.

===Chinese===
好一片船舶的世界！桅樯沿着城沿织成一带密林，所有的船都头朝街面停泊，像食槽边的马；锚泊的船只几乎堵死了通往更深处海湾的航道。这里有衰朽的西班牙的红黄条纹旗；有「伟大的民族」那蓝白红三色旗——血染到指尖；有王室联邦的联合十字旗；有伟大共和国的星条旗，还有荷兰和葡萄牙的、北意大利各邦的、巴西的以及西属大陆各共和国的几面旗子。我们在这些船中间缓慢而小心地穿行，从一艘战列舰的舷侧下、一艘螺旋桨快速帆舰的船尾下驶过——两舰都挂着西班牙旗——最后在雷格拉湾抛锚，泊在我们前面几天从纽约出发的「卡纳克」号（*Karnac*）轮船旁边。顷刻之间我们便被小船团团围住：有的满载橘子和香蕉，有的是来接乘客和行李的，船尾都张着凉篷，摇桨的是些面色苍黄、形容消瘦的男人，穿蓝白格子衣、戴草帽，其中不时可见黑人那副熟识的嘴唇和牙齿、那副容易满足的茫然面孔。这些船中间驶来一条，船尾飘着红黄两色、旗面上缀着王冠的旗子，凉篷下斜倚着一个穿全套白色亚麻衣的人，戴着草帽、红帽章，叼着雪茄。这是检疫官。在他满意之前，谁也不能登船，也不能离船。布洛克船长行礼，步下舷梯登上小艇，呈上文件，报告一切正常——我们被宣布安全。接着又来了一条同样气派的船，另一个男人斜倚在凉篷下叼着雪茄，他登上船来，与事务长密谈，把乘客名单同护照逐一核对，我们被宣布完全放行，获准携带行李在海关码头上岸。

===Original===
Now comes the war of cries and gestures and grimaces among the boatmen, in their struggle for passengers, increased manifold by the fact that there is but little language in common between the parties to the bargains, and by the boatmen being required to remain in their boats. How thin these boatmen look. You cannot get it out of your mind that they must all have had the yellow fever last summer, and are not yet fully recovered. Not only their faces, but their hands and arms and legs are thin, and their low-quartered slippers only half cover their thin yellow feet.

In the hurry, I have to hunt after the passengers I am to take leave of who go on to New Orleans:﻿—Mr. and Mrs. Benchley, on their way to their intended new home in Western Texas, my two sea captains, and the little son of my friend, who is the guest, on this voyage, of our common friend the captain, and after all, I miss the hearty handshake of Bullock and Rodgers. Seated under an awning, in the stern of a boat, with my trunk and carpetbag and an unseasonable bundle of Arctic overcoat and fur cap in the bow, I am pulled by a man with an oar in each hand and a cigar in mouth, to the customhouse pier. Here is a busy scene of trunks, carpet bags, and bundles; and up and down the pier, marches a military grandee of about the rank of a sergeant or sublieutenant, with a preposterous strut, so out of keeping with the depressed military character of his country, and not possible to be appreciated without seeing it. If he would give that strut on the boards, in New York, he would draw full houses nightly.

Our passports are kept, and we receive a license to remain and travel in the island, good for three months only, for which a large fee is paid. These officers of the customs are civil and reasonably rapid; and in a short time my luggage is on a dray driven by a Negro, and I am in a volante, managed by a Negro postilion, and am driving through the narrow streets of this surprising city.

===Chinese===
接着便是船夫们争抢乘客的叫喊、手势与鬼脸之战，而买卖双方几乎没有共通的语言，船夫又奉命不得离船，这战争便闹大了十倍。这些船夫看上去多瘦啊。你总免不了想：他们去年夏天准都害过黄热病，到现在还没大好。不光是脸，他们的手、胳膊和腿都是瘦的，低帮拖鞋只半遮着瘦黄的双脚。

忙乱之中，我还得去找那些要道别的人——他们要继续去新奥尔良：本奇利夫妇，正在前往他们打算在西得克萨斯建立的新家的路上；我的两位船长朋友；还有我朋友的小儿子，这次航行他是我们共同的朋友船长的客人；末了，我竟错过了与布洛克和罗杰斯的热烈握手。我坐在一条小船船尾的凉篷下，身旁是我的衣箱和毡毯提包，船头堆着不合时令的一捆北极皮大氅和皮帽，一个双手各执一桨、嘴里叼着雪茄的汉子把我摇到海关码头。这里是一派忙碌的景象，衣箱、提包、包裹来来往往；码头上走来走去的，是一位军界大人物，军衔大约相当于中士或少尉，迈着一种荒唐的阔步，同他那国家低沉的军人气概全不相称，不亲眼看一看简直无法领会。他若肯把那副步态搬到纽约的戏台上，准能夜夜满座。

我们的护照被扣下，领到一张准许居留和游历本岛的执照，有效期只三个月，为此付了一笔不小的费用。这些海关官员客气而手脚还算麻利；不多时，我的行李已装上一辆由黑人赶着的运货马车，我人也坐进一辆由黑人骑辕驭者驾驭的沃兰特马车（volante），正穿行在这座令人惊异的城市的大街小巷里。

===Original===
The streets are so narrow and the houses built so close upon them, that they seem to be rather spaces between the walls of houses than highways for travel. It appears impossible that two vehicles should pass abreast; yet they do so. There are constant blockings of the way. In some places awnings are stretched over the entire street, from house to house, and we are riding under a long tent. What strange vehicles these volantes are!﻿—A pair of very long, limber shafts, at one end of which is a pair of huge wheels, and at the other end a horse with his tail braided and brought forward and tied to the saddle, an open chaise body resting on the shafts, about one third of the way from the axle to the horse; and on the horse is a Negro, in large postilion boots, long spurs, and a bright jacket. It is an easy vehicle to ride in; but it must be a sore burden to the beast. Here and there we pass a private volante, distinguished by rich silver mountings and postilions in livery. Some have two horses, and with the silver and the livery and the long dangling traces and a look of superfluity, have rather an air of high life. In most, a gentleman is reclining, cigar in mouth; while in others, is a great puff of blue or pink muslin or cambric, extending over the sides to the shafts, topped off by a fan, with signs of a face behind it. “Calle de los Officios,” “Calle del Obispo,” “Calle de San Ignacio,” “Calle de Mercaderes,” are on the little corner boards. Every little shop and every big shop has its title; but nowhere does the name of a keeper appear. Almost every shop advertises “*por mayor y menor*,” wholesale and retail. What a *Gil Blas*, *Don Quixote* feeling the names of “posada,” “tienda,” and “cantina” give you!

===Chinese===
街道那么窄，房屋又那么紧贴着街修建，以致它们与其说是通衢大道，不如说是房屋墙壁之间的空隙。两辆车似乎不可能并排通过；然而居然过得去。道路时常堵塞。有些地方凉篷从这家屋顶连到那家屋顶，横跨整条街，我们便像在一条长帐篷底下行驶。这沃兰特马车是多么奇怪的车啊！——一对很长而柔韧的车辕，一端装着一对巨大的车轮，另一端是一匹尾巴编成辫、拉到前面拴在鞍上的马；敞篷的车身架在车辕上，约在车轴到马之间的三分之一处；马背上骑着个黑人，登着大号的驭者长靴、长长的马刺，穿一件鲜亮的短上衣。坐这种车很舒服；可对牲口准是一副沉重的苦役。不时有一辆私家的沃兰特驶过，以贵重的银饰和穿号衣的驭者见称。有的用两匹马，加上银饰、号衣和长长垂荡的挽索，一派富贵气象。多数车里斜倚着一位绅士，嘴里叼着雪茄；另一些车里则是一大团蓝色或粉红的细麻纱或细葛布，从车身两侧一直漫到车辕，顶上收着一柄扇子，扇后隐隐露出些许面孔。「官吏街」（Calle de los Officios）、「主教街」（Calle del Obispo）、「圣伊格纳西奥街」（Calle de San Ignacio）、「商人街」（Calle de Mercaderes），就写在小街角牌上。每家小铺、大铺都有自己的字号；可是哪儿也见不到店东的名字。几乎每家店都标着「*por mayor y menor*」——批发兼零售。「posada」（客店）、「tienda」（店铺）、「cantina」（酒馆）这些名字，给你一种多么浓的《吉尔·布拉斯》、《堂吉诃德》的味道！

===Original===
There are no women walking in the streets, except Negresses. Those suits of seersucker, with straw hats and red cockades, are soldiers. It is a sensible dress for the climate. Every third man, perhaps more, and not a few women, are smoking cigars or *cigarritos*. Here are things moving along, looking like cocks of new mown grass, underway. But presently you see the head of a horse or mule peering out from under the mass, and a tail is visible at the other end, and feet are picking their slow way over the stones. These are the carriers of green fodder, the fresh cut stalks and blades of corn; and my chance companion in the carriage, a fellow passenger by the *Cahawba*, a Frenchman, who has been here before, tells me that they supply all the horses and mules in the city with their daily feed, as no hay is used. There are also mules, asses, and horses with bananas, plantains, oranges and other fruits in panniers reaching almost to the ground.

===Chinese===
街上没有女人行走，黑人妇女除外。那些穿泡泡纱衣、戴草帽缀红帽章的是士兵。这身装束倒合此地气候。大约每三个人里——也许还不止——便有一个，而且不少女人也在抽雪茄或纸烟（*cigarritos*）。这边有些东西一路移动过来，看着像一堆堆新割的青草在行进。可一会儿你就看见一颗马头或骡头从草堆下面探出来，另一头还露出条尾巴，四只蹄子在石头路上慢挑慢拣地走着。这些是青饲料的运送者——新割的玉米秆和叶；我车里萍水相逢的同伴——「卡哈巴」号上同船的旅客、一位从前到过此地的法国人——告诉我，城里所有的骡马都靠它们供应每日的草料，因为这里不用干草。还有骡、驴和马，驮着香蕉、大蕉、橘子和其他水果，驮篮几乎垂到地上。

===Original===
Here is the Plaza de Armas, with its garden of rich, fragrant flowers in full bloom, in front of the Governor’s Palace. At the corner is the chapel erected over the spot where, under the auspices of Columbus, mass was first celebrated on the island. We are driven past a gloomy convent, past innumerable shops, past drinking places, billiard rooms, and the thick, dead walls of houses, with large windows, grated like dungeons, and large gates, showing glimpses of interior courtyards, sometimes with trees and flowers. But horses and carriages and gentlemen and ladies and slaves, all seem to use the same entrance. The windows come to the ground, and, being flush with the street, and mostly without glass, nothing but the grating prevents a passenger from walking into the rooms. And there the ladies and children sit sewing, or lounging, or playing. This is all very strange. There is evidently enough for me to see in the ten or twelve days of my stay.

But there are no costumes among the men, no Spanish hats, or Spanish cloaks, or bright jackets, or waistcoats, or open, slashed trousers, that are so picturesque in other Spanish countries. The men wear black dress coats, long pantaloons, black cravats, and many of them even submit, in this hot sun, to black French hats. The tyranny of systematic, scientific, capable, unpicturesque, unimaginativa France, evidently rules over the realm of man’s dress. The houses, the vehicles, the vegetation, the animals, are picturesque; to the eye of taste

> “Every prospect pleases, and only man is vile.”

===Chinese===
这就是武器广场（Plaza de Armas），总督府前满园浓香的花正盛开着。街角有一座小教堂，建在当年在哥伦布主持下、全岛首次举行弥撒的原址上。我们驶过一座阴沉的修道院，驶过数不清的店铺，驶过酒肆、弹子房，驶过厚实沉闷的宅墙——大窗装着囚牢般的铁栅，大门里隐约可见内院，有时还有花木。可是骡马与车驾、绅士与贵妇、奴隶，似乎都走同一个门。窗户直落地面，与街面齐平，大多不装玻璃，若不是那道铁栅，行人一抬脚就能走进屋里去。而女士们和孩子们就坐在那儿做针线，或闲倚着，或玩耍。这一切都非常新奇。在我逗留的十来天里，显然有足够我看的东西。

可是男人们身上没有什么民族装束，没有西班牙帽，没有西班牙斗篷，没有鲜亮的短上衣、背心，也没有别的一些西班牙国度里那么富有画意的开衩裤。男人们穿黑色礼服大衣、长裤、黑领结，许多人甚至顶着这烈日，忍受黑色的法国帽子。那讲体系、讲科学、能干而无画意、无想象力的法兰西的专制，显然统治着男装的王国。房屋、车辆、草木、牲畜，都有画意；在有品位的眼睛看来——

> 「处处景色宜人，唯人是卑劣。」

===Original===
We drove through the Puerta de Monserrate, a heavy gateway of the prevailing yellow or tawny color, where soldiers are on guard, across the moat, out upon the “Paseo de Isabel Segunda,” and are now “*estramuros*,” without the walls. The Paseo is a grand avenue running across the city from sea to bay, with two carriage-drives abreast, and two malls for foot passengers, and all lined with trees in full foliage. Here you catch a glimpse of the Morro, and there of the Presidio. This is the Teatro de Tacon; and, in front of this line of tall houses, in contrast with the almost uniform one-story buildings of the city, the volante stops. This is Le Grand’s hotel.

===Chinese===
我们驶出蒙塞拉特门（Puerta de Monserrate）——一座当地流行的黄色即黄褐色的厚重城门，门前有士兵把守——过了壕沟，登上「伊莎贝尔二世林荫大道」（Paseo de Isabel Segunda），此刻我们已在「*estramuros*」（城墙外）了。这条大道是一条从海到湾横贯全城的壮丽林荫道，两条车路并行，另有两条供步行的林荫带，两旁尽是枝叶繁茂的树木。这里可以瞥见莫罗，那里可以望见要塞监狱。这是塔康剧院（Teatro de Tacon）；在这一排高楼前面——与城里几乎清一色的一层平房恰成对照——沃兰特车停了下来。这就是勒格朗饭店（Le Grand's hotel）。

## IX The Belen﻿—The Jesuit college, brethren, and pupils﻿—The Order of Jesuits / 九　贝伦——耶稣会学院、会士与学生——耶稣会修会

===Original===
*Monday, February 2*.﻿—Rose before six and walked as usual, down the Paseo, to the sea baths. How refreshing is this bath, after the hot night and close rooms! At your side, the wide blue sea with its distant sails, the bath cut into the clean rock, the gentle washing in and out of the tideless sea, at the Gulf Stream temperature, in the cool of the morning! As I pass down, I meet a file of Coolies, in Chinese costume, marching, under overseers, to their work or their jail. And there is the chain gang! clank, clank, as they go headed by officers with pistols and swords, and flanked by drivers with whips. This is simple wretchedness!

===Chinese===
*2 月 21 日，星期一*。——六点前起身，照常沿林荫大道走到海边浴场。经过炎热的夜晚和憋闷的房间，这一澡多么解乏！身边是宽阔的蓝海和远处的帆影，浴池凿在洁净的岩石里，无潮的海水温和地流进流出，水温是湾流的温度，又正当清晨的凉爽！走去时，我迎面遇到一列华工，穿着中国服装，在监工押带下行进，去上工或是回监狱。还有锁链苦役队！哐啷、哐啷，他们走过来了，前头是佩手枪挎军刀的军官，两侧是执鞭的赶工。这就是赤裸裸的悲惨！

===Original===
While at breakfast, a gentleman in the dress of the regular clergy, speaking English called upon me, bringing me, from the bishop an open letter of introduction and admission to all the religious, charitable, and educational institutions of the city, and offering to conduct me to the Belen (Bethlehem). He is Father B. of Charleston, SC, temporarily in Havana, with whom I find I have some acquaintances in common, both in America and abroad. We drive together to the Belen. I say drive; for few persons walk far in Havana, after ten o’clock in the morning. The volantes are the public carriages of Havana; and are as abundant as cabs in London. You never need stand long at a street door without finding one. The postilions are always Negroes; and I am told that they pay the owner a certain sum per day for the horse and volante, and make what they can above that.

===Chinese===
正在用早餐，一位穿正规教士服装、说英语的绅士来访；他带来主教给我的一封公开介绍信，凭它可以参观城中一切宗教、慈善和教育机构，并提出带我去贝伦（Belen，即「伯利恒」）。他是南卡罗来纳州查尔斯顿的 B 神父（Father B.），暂时在哈瓦那；我发现我们在美洲和海外都有几个共同的熟人。我们一同驱车去贝伦。我说驱车；因为在哈瓦那，上午十点以后很少有人走远路。沃兰特就是哈瓦那的公共马车，多得像伦敦的街车。你在哪家门口都不必久等就会等到一辆。骑辕的驭者总是黑人；听说他们每天向车主付一笔固定的钱，租下马和车，多挣的归自己。

===Original===
The Belen is a group of buildings, of the usual yellow or tawny color, covering a good deal of ground, and of a thoroughly monastic character. It was first a Franciscan monastery, then a barrack, and now has been given by the Government to the Jesuits. The company of Jesuits here is composed of a rector and about forty clerical and twenty lay brethren. These perform every office, from the highest scientific investigations and instruction, down to the lowest menial offices, in the care of the children; some serving in costly vestments at the high altar, and others in coarse black garb at the gates. It is only three years since they established themselves in Havana, but in that time they have formed a school of two hundred boarders and one hundred day scholars, built dormitories for the boarders, and a common hall, restored the church and made it the most fully attended in the city; established a missionary work in all parts of the town, recalled a great number to the discipline of the Church, and not only created something like an enthusiasm of devotion among the women, who are said to have monopolized the religion of Cuba in times past, but have introduced among the men, and among many influential men, the practices of confession and communion, to which they had been almost entirely strangers. I do not take this account from the Jesuits themselves, but from the regular clergy of other orders, and from Protestants who are opposed to them and their influence. All agree that they are at work with zeal and success.

===Chinese===
贝伦是一组建筑，惯常的黄色即黄褐色，占地很广，一派十足的修道院气象。它先是方济各会修道院，后做过兵营，如今由政府拨给了耶稣会。这里的耶稣会士团体由一位院长、约四十位神职会士和二十位辅理修士组成。从最高深的科学研究与教学，直到照料孩童的最卑微的杂役，他们都亲力亲为；有的穿着华贵的祭衣在高坛侍奉，有的穿着粗黑布衣守在大门口。他们定居哈瓦那不过三年，却在这段时间里办起了一所有两百名寄宿生、一百名走读生的学校，为寄宿生盖了宿舍和一座公共礼堂，修复了教堂，使之成为城中上座最满的一座；在全城各处建立了传教事业，把大批人召回教会的训导之下；不仅在没有女人中间——据说往昔古巴的宗教一向由女人独占——唤起了一种近乎狂热的热忱，还在男人中间、在许多有势力的男人中间，推行了告解和领圣体的功课，而这些功课他们先前几乎全然陌生。这份陈述并非取自耶稣会士自己，而是取自其他修会的正规教士，以及反对他们及其影响的新教徒。众口一词：他们正以热忱和成效工作着。

===Original===
I met my distinguished acquaintance of yesterday, the rector, who took me to the boys’ chapel, and introduced me to Father Antonio Cabre, a very young man of a spare frame and intellectual countenance, with hands so white and so thin, and eyes so bright, and cheek so pale! He is at the head of the department of mathematics and astronomy, and looks indeed as if he had outwatched the stars, in vigils of science or of devotion. He took me to his laboratory, his observatory, and his apparatus of philosophic instruments. These I am told are according to the latest inventions, and in the best style of French and German workmanship. I was also shown a collection of coins and medals, a cabinet of shells, the commencement of a museum of natural history, already enriched with most of the birds of Cuba, and an interesting cabinet of the woods of the island, in small blocks, each piece being polished on one side, and rough on the other. Among the woods were the mahoganies, the ironwood, the ebony, the lignum vitæ, the cedar, and many others, of names unfamiliar to me, which admit of the most exquisite polish. Some of the most curious were from the Isla de Pinos, an island belonging to Cuba, and on its southern shore.

===Chinese===
我遇见了昨日结识的那位名人——院长，他带我到学生小堂，把我介绍给安东尼奥·卡布雷神父（Father Antonio Cabre）。他很年轻，清瘦的身架，一张读书人的脸，一双手那么白、那么细，眼睛那么亮，脸颊那么苍白！他主持数学和天文的教学，看那样子真像是为了科学或敬神的守夜，把星星都守得熬过去了。他带我去看他的实验室、观象台和他的哲学仪器设备。据说这些都是依照最新的发明，出自法国和德国工艺的最佳风格。还给我看了钱币和徽章的收藏、一柜贝壳、一座初具规模的自然史博物馆——已经收有古巴大部分鸟类——还有一座很有意思的本岛木材陈列柜，都是小块木料，每块一面抛光，一面留毛。木材中有桃花心木、铁木、乌木、愈疮木、香柏，还有许多我叫不上名字的，都能打磨出最精美的光泽。几种最奇特的出自松树岛（Isla de Pinos）——古巴属下的一座岛，在古巴的南海岸外。

===Original===
The sleeping arrangement for the boys here seemed to me to be new, and to be well adapted to the climate. There is a large hall, with a roof about thirty feet from the floor, and windows near the top, to give light and ventilation above, and small portholes, near the ground, to let air into the passages. In this hall are double rows of compartments, like high pews, or, more profanely, like the large boxes in restaurants and chophouses, open at the top, with curtains instead of doors, and each large enough to contain a single bed, a chair, and a toilet table. This ensures both privacy and the light and air of the great hall. The bedsteads are of iron; and nothing can exceed the neatness and order of the apartments. The boys’ clothes are kept in another part of the house, and they take to their dormitories only the clothes that they are using. Each boy sleeps alone. Several of the Fathers sleep in the hall, in curtained rooms at the ends of the passageways, and a watchman walks the rounds all night, to guard against fire, and to give notice of sickness.

The boys have a playground, a gymnasium, and a riding school. But although they like riding and fencing, they do not take to the robust exercises and sports of English school boys. An American whom I met here, who had spent several months at the school, told me that in their recreations they were more like girls, and liked to sit a good deal, playing or working with their hands. He pointed out to me a boy, the son of an American mother, a lady to whom I brought letters and kind wishes from her many friends at the North, and told me that he had more pluck than any boy in the school.

The roof of the Belen is flat, and gives a pleasant promenade, in the open air, after the sun is gone down, which is much needed, as the buildings are in the dense part of the city.

===Chinese===
这里男童的睡卧安排，在我看来颇为新奇，也很适合此地气候。有一座大厅，屋顶离地约三十英尺，窗子开在近顶处，从上面采光通风，近地面处开小孔，给过道进气。厅内是成双排列的小隔间，像高高的教堂包座，或者更俗气地说，像餐馆和小饭馆里的大格间：顶上敞开，以帘代门，每间恰好放一张床、一把椅子、一张梳妆台。这样既得私密，又享大厅的光线和空气。床架是铁的；居处的整洁与条理无以复加。男孩们的衣物放在楼里另一处，他们只把正穿用的带进宿舍。每个男孩单独睡。有几位神父也睡在厅里，住在过道两端垂帘的小间里，一名值守者整夜巡查，以防火灾，也照应疾病。

男童们有游戏场、健身房和骑术学校。可是他们虽然喜欢骑马和击剑，却不喜欢英国学童那种剽悍的运动和游戏。我在这里遇到一位美国人，在这所学校待过几个月；他告诉我，这些孩子玩耍起来更像女孩，喜欢久坐，动手玩些东西或做些活计。他指给我看一个男孩——一位美国母亲的儿子，我正带着她北方许多朋友的书信和问候要捎给她——他说这孩子的胆气胜过全校任何一个男孩。

贝伦的屋顶是平的，太阳落山后在露天上面散步颇为惬意；这很必要，因为这些建筑处在城中稠密的地段。

===Original===
The brethren of this order wear short hair, with the tonsure, and dress in coarse cassocks of plain black, coming to the feet, and buttoned close to the neck, with a cape, but with no white of collar above; and in these, they sweep like black spectres, about the passageways, and across the halls and courtyards. There are so many of them that they are able to give thorough and minute attention to the boys, not only in instruction, both secular and religious, but in their entire training and development.

From the scholastic part of the institution, I passed to the church. It is not very large, has an open marble floor, a gallery newly erected for the use of the brethren and other men, a sumptuous high altar, a sacristy and vestry behind, and a small altar, by which burned the undying lamp, indicating the presence of the Sacrament. In the vestry, I was shown the vestments for the service of the high altar, some of which are costly and gorgeous in the extreme, not probably exceeded by those of the Temple at Jerusalem in the palmiest days of the Jewish hierarchy. All are presents from wealthy devotees. One, an alb, had a circle of precious stones; and the lace alone on another, a present from a lady of rank, is said to have cost three thousand dollars. Whatever may be thought of the rightfulness of this expenditure, turning upon the old question as to which the alabaster box of ointment and the ordained costliness of the Jewish ritual “must give us pause,” it cannot be said of the Jesuits that they live in cedar, while the ark of God rests in curtains; for the actual life of the streets hardly presents any greater contrast, than that between the sumptuousness of their apparel at the altar, and the coarseness and cheapness of their ordinary dress, the bareness of their rooms, and the apparent severity of their life.

===Chinese===
这个修会的会士留短发，带剃发圈，穿粗布的素黑长袍，长及脚面，领口扣严，带披肩，领上没有白领；穿着这一身，他们像黑色的幽灵似的在过道里、厅院间掠来掠去。他们人数众多，因此能够对男童们给予透彻而细致的照管，不仅在于世俗和宗教的课业，也在于他们的整个训练与成长。

从学校的部分，我走到教堂。教堂不算很大，有敞开的大理石地面，新添了一层楼座供会士和其他男士使用，一座华丽的高坛，后面是圣器室和祭衣房，还有一座小祭坛，坛前点着不灭的长明灯，标示圣体的临在。在祭衣房里，人家给我看高坛祭礼用的祭衣，有几件华贵已极，恐怕犹太祭司制度全盛之日耶路撒冷圣殿里的也不见得胜过。都是豪富信众的馈赠。其中一件白祭袍（alb）镶着一圈宝石；另一件的花边——一位贵妇所赠——据说就花了 3000 美元。无论如何看待这笔开支的正当与否——它引出那个老问题：玉瓶里的香膏、犹太仪礼法定的靡费，「必使我等踌躇」——但耶稣会士是不能被指为「自己住香柏木的宫中，却让神的约柜安在幔子里」的；因为街市人生的实际，几乎再没有比这更大的反差了：他们在坛上的衣着何等华贵，平日的衣着便何等粗陋价廉，居室何等空无，生活表面何等严苛。

===Original===
The Cubans have a childish taste for excessive decoration. Their altars look like toy shops. A priest, not a Cuban, told me that he went to the high altar of the cathedral once, on a Christmas day, to officiate, and when his eye fell on the childish and almost profane attempts at symbolism﻿—a kind of doll millinery﻿—if he had not got so far that he could not retire without scandal, he would have left the duties of the day to others. At the Belen there is less of this; but the Jesuits find or think it necessary to conform a good deal to the popular taste.

In the sacristy, near the side altar, is a distressing image of the Virgin, not in youth, but the mother of the mature man, with a sword pierced through her heart﻿—referring to the figurative prediction, “a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also.” The handle and a part of the blade remain without, while the marks of the deep wound are seen, and the countenance expresses the sorest agony of mind and body. It is painful, and beyond all legitimate scope of art, and haunts one, like a vision of actual misery. It is almost the only thing in the church of which I have brought away a distinct image in my memory.

===Chinese===
古巴人对过度的装饰有一种稚气的趣味。他们的祭坛看着像玩具店。一位并非古巴人的司铎告诉我，某年圣诞节他到大教堂的高坛主持礼仪，一眼看见那些稚气的、近乎亵渎的象征手法——一种玩偶女帽铺的做派——若不是已经走到不闹出丑闻便无法退出的地步，他真要把当天的职分让给别人了。贝伦这方面较少；但耶稣会士发现、或者认为有必要在相当程度上迎合大众的趣味。

圣器室里，小祭坛旁，有一尊令人心碎的圣母像：不是青春的圣母，而是成人之子的母亲，一柄剑穿透了她的心——应着那句喻言的预言：「你自己的心也要被刀刺透。」剑柄和一段剑身留在外面，深创的痕迹历历可见，面容表达着心灵与肉体至深的痛楚。它是惨痛的，越出了艺术一切正当的范围，像一个真实苦难的幻象般纠缠着人。这几乎是这座教堂里唯一一件在我记忆中留下清晰形象的东西。

===Original===
A strange, eventful history, is that of the Society of Jesus! Ignatius Loyola, a soldier and noble of Spain, renouncing arms and knighthood, hangs his trophies of war upon the altar of Monserrate. After intense studies and barefoot pilgrimages, persecuted by religious orders whose excesses he sought to restrain, and frowned upon by the Inquisition he organizes, with Xavier and Faber, at Montmartre, a society of three. From this small beginning, spreading upwards and outwards, it overshadows the earth. Now, at the top of success, it is supposed to control half Christendom. Now, his order proscribed by State and Church alike and suppressed by the Pope himself, there is not a spot of earth in Catholic Christendom where the Jesuit can place the sole of his foot. In this hour of distress, he finds refuge in Russia, and in Protestant Prussia. Then, restored and tolerated, the order revives here and there in Europe, with a fitful life; and, at length, blazes out into a glory of missionary triumphs and martyrdoms in China, in India, in Africa, and in North America; and now, in these later days, we see it advancing everywhere to a new epoch of labor and influence. Thorough in education, perfect in discipline, absolute in obedience﻿—as yielding, as indestructible, as all-pervading as water or as air!

===Chinese===
耶稣会的历史，是一部多么离奇、多事的历史！依纳爵·罗耀拉，西班牙的军人与贵族，舍弃兵戈与骑士之名，把他的战利品挂上蒙塞拉特的圣坛。经过苦修的研读和赤足的朝圣，又受他力图约束其放恣的各修会的迫害、遭他效力效力的宗教裁判所的白眼，他在蒙马特与沙勿略、法伯尔一起，创立了一个三人的会社。从这小小的开端，向上向外铺展，荫覆了大地。曾几何时，在成功的极顶，据说它左右着半个基督教世界。曾几何时，他的修会遭国家与教会一齐禁绝，连教皇亲自将它解散，公教基督教世界竟没有一寸土地容耶稣会士立足。在这患难的时辰，他在俄国、在新教的普鲁士找到庇护。随后，恢复与容许之下，修会在欧洲各地带着断续的生命复活；终于，在中国、在印度、在非洲、在北美，迸发出传教的凯旋与殉道的光焰；而如今，在晚近的这些日子里，我们看见它到处向着一个劳作与影响的新纪元迈进。教育上的彻底，纪律上的完美，服从上的绝对——像水又像空气一般柔顺、不灭、无所不入！

===Original===
The Jesuits make strong friends and strong enemies. Many, who are neither the one nor the other, say of them that their ethics are artificial, and their system unnatural; that they do not reform nature, but destroy it; that, aiming to use the world without abusing it, they reduce it to subjection and tutelage; that they are always either in dangerous power, or in disgrace; and although they may labor with more enthusiasm and self-consecration than any other order, and meet with astonishing successes for a time, yet such is the character of their system that these successes are never permanent, but result in opposition, not only from Protestants, and moderate Catholics, and from the civil power, but from other religious orders and from the regular clergy in their own Church﻿—an opposition to which they are invariably compelled to yield, at last. In fine, they declare, that, allowing them all zeal, and all ability, and all devotedness, their system is too severe and too unnatural for permanent usefulness anywhere﻿—medicine and not food, lightning and not light, flame and not warmth.

===Chinese===
耶稣会士交结下坚定的朋友，也结下坚定的敌人。许多既非友亦非敌的人这样说他们：他们的伦理是矫揉造作的，他们的制度是违逆天性的；他们不改良天性，而是摧折天性；他们本想用世而不溺于世，结果把世界置于辖制与监护之下；他们不是身处危险的权势，便是身处声名狼藉；虽然他们可以比任何别的修会更能以热忱和忘我效力，也能一时收惊人成功之效，然而他们的制度性质使然，这些成功从无长久，到头来招致反对——不仅来自新教徒、温和派公教徒和世俗政权，也来自他们自己教会里的其他修会和正规教士——这反对他们终究不得不屈服。总而言之，这些人断言：纵然给他们加上全部的热忱、全部的才干、全部的献身精神，他们的制度也过于严苛、过于违反天性，在任何地方都难有持久的用处——是药石而非粮食，是电闪而非光明，是火焰而非温暖。

===Original===
Not satisfied with this moderated judgment, their opponents have met them, always and everywhere, with uniform and vehement reprobation. They say to them﻿—the opinion of mankind has condemned you! The just and irreversible sentence of time has made you a byword and a hissing, and reduced your very name, the most sacred in its origin, to a synonyme for ambition and deceit!

Others, again, esteem them the nearest approach in modern times to that type of men portrayed by one of the chiefest, in his epistle: “In much patience, in afflictions, in necessities, in distresses, in stripes, in imprisonments, in tumults, in labors, in watchings, in fastings; by pureness, by knowledge, by long suffering;﻿… by honor and dishonor; by evil report and good report; as deceivers and yet true; as unknown, and yet well known; as dying, and behold we live; as chastened, and not killed; as sorrowful, and yet always rejoicing; as poor, yet making many rich; as having nothing, and yet possessing all things.”

===Chinese===
他们的对手并不满足于这种有节制的评判，还要始终处处以一律而激烈的痛斥相向。他们对他们说——人类的公论已经判了你们的罪！时间那公正而不移的判决，已使你们成为笑柄与嘘声，把你们那本源最神圣的名字，贬成了野心与欺诈的同义词！

另一些人则敬重他们，认为他们是近代最接近那种人格典型的人——那典型是一位使徒之首在他的书信里描画过的：「许多的忍耐、患难、穷乏、困苦、鞭打、监禁、扰乱、勤劳、儆醒、不食；廉洁、知识、恒忍……荣耀羞辱，恶名美名；似乎是诱惑人的，却是诚实的；似乎不为人所知，却是人所共知的；似乎要死，却是活着的；似乎受责罚，却是不至丧命的；似乎忧愁，却是常常快乐的；似乎贫穷，却是叫许多人富足的；似乎一无所有，却是样样都有的。」

## V Cuban hotel, and its landlord, rooms, servants, and guests﻿—Breakfast﻿—Fresh fruits﻿—Houses and counting rooms of merchants and bankers﻿—Dr. Howe and Mr. Parker﻿—Dinner﻿—Opera troupe﻿—Speech of the Cubans﻿—Afternoon on the Paseo﻿—*Retreta* at the Plaza de Armas﻿—Havana by night / 五　古巴的旅馆及其店主、房间、仆役与客人——早餐——鲜果——商人与银行家的宅邸和账房——豪博士与帕克先生——正餐——歌剧团——古巴人的谈吐——午后的林荫大道——武器广场的晚乐会——夜哈瓦那

===Original===
To a person unaccustomed to the tropics or the south of Europe, I know of nothing more discouraging than the arrival at the inn or hotel. It is nobody’s business to attend to you. The landlord is strangely indifferent, and if there is a way to get a thing done, you have not learned it, and there is no one to teach you. Le Grand is a Frenchman. His house is a restaurant, with rooms for lodgers. The restaurant is paramount. The lodging is secondary, and is left to servants. Monsieur does not condescend to show a room, even to families; and the servants, who are whites, but mere lads, have all the interior in their charge, and there are no women employed about the chambers. Antonio, a swarthy Spanish lad, in shirt sleeves, looking very much as if he never washed, has my part of the house in charge, and shows me my room. It has but one window, a door opening upon the veranda, and a brick floor, and is very bare of furniture, and the furniture has long ceased to be strong. A small stand barely holds up a basin and ewer which have not been washed since Antonio was washed, and the bedstead, covered by a canvas sacking, without mattress or bed, looks as if it would hardly bear the weight of a man. It is plain there is a good deal to be learned here. Antonio is communicative, on a suggestion of several days’ stay and good pay. Things which we cannot do without, we must go out of the house to find, and those which we can do without, we must dispense with. This is odd, and strange, but not uninteresting, and affords scope for contrivance and the exercise of influence and other administrative powers. The Grand Seigneur does not mean to be troubled with anything; so there are no bells, and no office, and no clerks. He is the only source, and if he is approached, he shrugs his shoulders and gives you to understand that you have your chambers for your money and must look to the servants. Antonio starts off on an expedition for a pitcher of water and a towel, with a faint hope of two towels; for each demand involves an expedition to remote parts of the house. Then Antonio has so many rooms dependent on him, that every door is a Scylla, and every window a Charybdis, as he passes. A shrill, female voice, from the next room but one, calls “Antonio! Antonio!” and that starts the parrot in the court yard, who cries “Antonio! Antonio!” for several minutes. A deep, bass voice mutters “Antonio!” in a more confidential tone; and last of all, an unmistakably Northern voice attempts it, but ends in something between Antonio and Anthony. He is gone a good while, and has evidently had several episodes to his journey. But he is a good-natured fellow, speaks a little French, very little English, and seems anxious to do his best.

===Chinese===
对一个不惯于热带或南欧生活的人，我知道再没有比抵达客栈或旅馆更叫人泄气的事了。没有人有照应你的义务。店主出奇地冷淡，即使事情有办成的门路，你也没有学会，也没人教你。勒格朗是法国人。他的房子是家餐馆，兼带客房。餐馆是第一位的，住宿是第二位的，交给仆人去管。这位先生不肯屈尊带你去看房间，即使对举家光顾的客人也一样；而那些仆人——都是白人，却不过是些小伙计——掌管着里面的一切，客房里不用一个女人。安东尼奥（Antonio），一个黑黢黢的西班牙小伙子，只穿衬衫，那副样子很像是从来不洗脸的，负责照管我住的这一片，领我看了我的房间。房间只有一扇窗，一道门通向游廊，砖地，家具简陋之至，而且那些家具早就谈不上结实了。一张小台子勉强托着一个脸盆和一只水壶——自打安东尼奥上次洗脸以来就没洗过——床架绷着帆布床屉，没有褥子也没有被，看上去几乎承不住一个人的重量。显然，这里有很不少东西要学。听到要住几天、报酬从优的暗示，安东尼奥便肯说话了。那些我们缺了不行的东西，必须出屋去另觅；那些缺了也行的，就必须将就着不用。这很古怪，很新奇，但并非无趣，还给了你施展心计、发挥影响力和种种行政才干的机会。这位大老爷不想为任何事情操心；所以没有铃，没有账房，也没有文书。他是唯一的源头，若有人径直找上他，他便耸耸肩，让你明白：你的房钱换来的是你的房间，其余得找仆人去。安东尼奥出发远征，去找一罐水和一条毛巾，还带着一线希望——两条毛巾；因为每样需求都要远征到房子的偏远角落。况且安东尼奥名下管着这么多房间，以至他每经过一扇门都是一个斯库拉，每扇窗都是一个卡律布狄斯。隔壁再隔壁的房里，一个尖利的女人声音喊「安东尼奥！安东尼奥！」于是院里的鹦鹉也跟着叫起「安东尼奥！安东尼奥！」，一叫好几分钟。一个深沉的男低音用更亲密的腔调咕哝着「安东尼奥！」；最后，一个一耳便知是北方人的口音也来试了试，结果发出一个介于安东尼奥与安东尼之间的声音。他去了好一阵，一路上显然经历了好几段插曲。但他是个和气的小伙子，会讲一点法语，英语只会一点点，看得出很想尽力。

===Original===
I see the faces of my New York fellow passengers from the west gallery, and we come together and throw our acquisitions of information into a common stock, and help one another. Mr. Miller’s servant, who has been here before, says there are baths and other conveniences round the corner of the street; and, sending our bundles of thin clothes there, we take advantage of the baths, with comfort. To be sure, we must go through a billiard room, where the Creoles are playing at the tables, and the cockroaches playing under them, and through a drinking room, and a bowling alley; but the baths are built in the open yard, protected by blinds, well ventilated, and well supplied with water and toilet apparatus.

With the comfort of a bath, and clothed in linen, with straw hats, we walk back to Le Grand’s, and enter the restaurant, for breakfast﻿—the breakfast of the country, at 10 o’clock. Here is a scene so pretty as quite to make up for the defects of the chambers. The restaurant with cool marble floor, walls twenty-four feet high, open rafters painted blue, great windows open to the floor and looking into the Paseo, and the floor nearly on a level with the street, a light breeze fanning the thin curtains, the little tables, for two or four, with clean white cloths, each with its pyramid of great red oranges and its fragrant bouquet﻿—the gentlemen in white pantaloons and jackets and white stockings, and the ladies in flyaway muslins, and hair in the sweet neglect of the morning toilet, taking their leisurely breakfasts of fruit and claret, and omelette and Spanish mixed dishes, (ollas) and café noir. How airy and ethereal it seems! They are birds, not substantial men and women. They eat ambrosia and drink nectar. It must be that they fly, and live in nests, in the tamarind trees. Who can eat a hot, greasy breakfast of cakes and gravied meats, and in a close room, after this?

===Chinese===
我在西边游廊上望见了纽约同船旅伴们的面孔，我们聚到一起，把各自打听来的门道汇成公用的智囊，彼此帮衬。米勒先生的仆人从前到过这里，说街角拐过去就有澡堂和其他便利；我们把薄衣包袱送去那里，舒舒服服洗了个澡。当然，我们得穿过一间弹子房——克里奥尔人在台子上打弹子，蟑螂在台子底下打——还得穿过一间酒室和一条保龄球道；但澡堂建在敞院里，有百叶窗遮护，通风良好，用水和梳洗设施也齐全。

洗过痛快的澡，换上亚麻衣、戴上草帽，我们走回勒格朗饭店，进餐厅用早餐——本地式的早餐，十点钟开。这里的景象如此美好，足可补偿客房的种种缺陷。餐厅是清凉的大理石地面，墙壁高二十四英尺，敞露的椽木漆成蓝色，大窗直落地面、望向林荫大道，地面几乎与街面相平；轻风拂动薄薄的窗帘；一张张小桌，坐两人或四人，铺着干净的白桌布，各摆着一座大红橘子垒成的金字塔和一束香花——绅士们穿白裤、白短上衣和白长袜，女士们穿着轻盈的细麻纱裙，头发带着晨妆那种甜美的慵散，从容地用着他们的早餐：水果和红葡萄酒、摊鸡蛋和西班牙什锦（olla），还有清咖啡。这一切显得多么轻盈、多么空灵！他们是鸟儿，不是有血有肉的男男女女。他们吃仙馔，饮琼浆。他们想必是会飞的，住在罗望子树上的巢里。在这之后，谁还能在闷热的房间里，吃一顿热腾腾、油腻腻的饼子和浇汁肉的早餐呢？

===Original===
I can truly say that I ate, this morning, my first orange; for I had never before eaten one newly gathered, which had ripened in the sun, hanging on the tree. We call for the usual breakfast, leaving the selection to the waiter; and he brings us fruits, claret, omelette, fish fresh from the sea, rice excellently cooked, fried plantains, a mixed dish of meat and vegetables (olla), and coffee. The fish, I do not remember its name, is boiled, and has the colors of the rainbow, as it lies on the plate. Havana is a good fish-market; for it is as open to the ocean as Nahant, or the beach at Newport; its streets running to the blue sea, outside the harbor, so that a man may almost throw his line from the curbstone into the Gulf Stream.

After breakfast, I take a volante and ride into the town, to deliver my letters. Three merchants whom I call upon, have palaces for their business. The entrances are wide, the staircases almost as stately as that of Stafford House, the floors of marble, the panels of porcelain tiles, the rails of iron, and the rooms over twenty feet high, with open rafters, the doors and windows colossal, the furniture rich and heavy; and there sits the merchant or banker, in white pantaloons and thin shoes and loose white coat and narrow necktie, smoking a succession of cigars, surrounded by tropical luxuries and tropical protections. In the lower story of one of these buildings is an exposition of silks, cotton and linens, in a room so large that it looked like a part of the Great Exhibition in Hyde Park. At one of these counting palaces, I met Mr. Theodore Parker and Dr. S. G. Howe, of Boston, who preceded me, in the *Karnac*. Mr. Parker is here for his health, which has caused anxiety to his friends lest his weakened frame should no longer support the strong intellectual machinery, as before. He finds Havana too hot, and will leave for Santa Cruz by the first opportunity. Dr. Howe likes the warm weather. It is a comfort to see him, a benefactor of his race, and one of the few heroes we have left to us, since Kane died.

===Chinese===
我可以认真地说，今天早晨我平生第一次吃到了橘子；因为我以前从未吃过一只新摘的、在太阳底下、挂在枝头成熟的橘子。我们照例点了早餐，菜色听凭侍者安排；他给我们端来水果、红葡萄酒、摊鸡蛋、刚出海的鲜鱼、烧得极好的米饭、炸大蕉、一盆肉与蔬菜合烩的菜（olla），还有咖啡。那鱼——我记不得名字了——是白煮的，卧在盘子里时浑身是彩虹的颜色。哈瓦那是个好鱼市；因为它同纳汉特、同纽波特的海滩一样直面大洋；街道一直通到港湾外的蓝色大海，人几乎可以从街沿石上把钓线甩进湾流里去。

早餐后，我雇了一辆沃兰特车进城送信。我拜访的三位商人，做生意用的是宫殿。门厅宽大，楼梯气派几乎不亚于斯塔福德宅邸，地面是大理石，墙板镶着瓷砖，栏杆是铁的，房间净高二十多英尺，椽木敞露，门窗都是巨型的，家具厚重华贵；商人或银行家就坐在那里，白裤子、薄底鞋、宽松的白外套、窄领结，一支接一支地抽着雪茄，被热带的奢华与热带的防护包围着。其中一座楼房的底层陈列着丝绸、棉布和麻布，房间大得像是海德公园万国博览会的一部分。在这样一座账房宫殿里，我遇见了西奥多·帕克先生（Mr. Theodore Parker）和豪博士（Dr. S. G. Howe），波士顿人，乘「卡纳克」号先我而至。帕克先生是为健康来此，他的状况一直令朋友们忧心，唯恐他孱弱的身体再也不能像从前那样支撑那台强劲的智力机器了。他觉得哈瓦那太热，一有机会就要动身去圣克鲁斯。豪博士倒喜欢暖天气。见到他是件快慰的事——他是人类的一位恩人，也是凯恩去世后我们仅存的几位英雄之一。

===Original===
The Bishop of Havana has been in delicate health, and is out of town, at Jesus del Monte; and Miss M﻿—﻿— is not at home, and the Señoras F﻿—﻿— I failed to see this morning; but I find a Boston young lady, whose friends were desirous I should see her, and who was glad enough to meet one so lately from her home. A clergyman to whom, also, I had letters, is gone into the country, without much hope of improving his health. Stepping into a little shop to buy a plan of Havana, my name is called, and there is my hero’s wife, the accomplished author and conversationist, whom it is an exhilaration to meet anywhere, much more in a land of strangers. Dr. and Mrs. Howe and Mr. Parker are at the Cerro, a pretty and cool place in the suburbs, but are coming in to Mrs. Almy’s boardinghouse, for the convenience of being in the city, and for nearness to friends, and the comforts of something like American or English housekeeping.

===Chinese===
哈瓦那主教身体欠安，出城到赫苏斯-德尔蒙特去了；M——小姐也不在家，F——几位夫人今晨我也没能见到；倒是遇到了一位波士顿的年轻女士，她的朋友们有心让我见见她，她见到一位刚从家乡来的人，也高兴得很。另一位我带着介绍信去访的牧师到乡下去了，康复的希望不大。踏进一家小店买哈瓦那地图时，有人叫我的名字——原来是我那位英雄的妻子，那位才华出众的作家和健谈的人，在哪儿遇到她都令人振奋，何况是在异邦。豪博士夫妇和帕克先生住在塞罗，郊外一处秀丽清凉的地方，但就要搬进城里的奥尔米太太公寓，图的是住在城里方便，离朋友们近，还有近乎美国或英国家常的那种舒适。

===Original===
In the latter part of the afternoon, from three o’clock, our parties are taking dinner at Le Grand’s. The little tables are again full, with a fair complement of ladies. The afternoon breeze is so strong that the draught of air, though it is hot air, is to be avoided. The passersby almost put their faces into the room, and the women and children of the poorer order look wistfully in upon the luxurious guests, the colored glasses, the red wines, and the golden fruits. The Opera troupe is here, both the singers and the ballet; and we have Gazzaniga, Lamoureux, Max Maretzek and his sister, and others, in this house, and Adelaide Phillips at the next door, and the benefit of a rehearsal, at nearly all hours of the day, of operas that the Habaneros are to rave over at night.

===Chinese===
下午后半段，从三点钟起，我们一伙人在勒格朗用正餐。小桌又坐满了，女士占了相当的数目。午后的风那么大，那股穿堂的气流——虽然吹的是热风——也须避开。过路人几乎把脸伸进屋来，穷人家的女人和孩子眼巴巴地望着里面奢华的客人、彩色玻璃杯、红色的酒和金黄的水果。歌剧团就在这里，唱歌的和跳舞的都有；我们这座楼里住着加扎尼加、拉穆勒、马克斯·马雷泽克和他的妹妹，还有其他各位；隔壁住着阿德莱德·菲利普斯；一天里几乎任何时辰都听得到排练——那些哈瓦那人晚上要为之如痴如狂的歌剧。

===Original===
I yield to no one in my admiration of the Spanish as a spoken language, whether in its rich, sonorous, musical, and lofty style, in the mouth of a man who knows its uses, or in the soft, indolent, languid tones of a woman, broken by an occasional birdlike trill﻿—

> “With wanton heed, and giddy cunning,
 

 The melting voice through mazes running”﻿—

but I do not like it as spoken by the common people of Cuba, in the streets. Their voices and intonations are thin and eager, very rapid, too much in the lips, and, withal, giving an impression of the passionate and the childish combined; and it strikes me that the tendency here is to enfeeble the language, and take from it the openness of the vowels and the strength of the harder consonants. This is the criticism of a few hours’ observation, and may not be just; but I have heard the same from persons who have been longer acquainted with it. Among the well educated Cubans, the standard of Castilian is said to be kept high, and there is a good deal of ambition to reach it.

===Chinese===
我对西班牙语作为口语的仰慕不逊于任何人——无论是深知其妙用的男子口中的那种丰厚、洪亮、乐感而高贵的格调，还是女子口中的那种轻柔、慵懒、无力的声调，不时被一声鸟鸣般的颤音打断——

> 「漫不经心而轻佻狡黠，
 

 醉人的嗓音在迷宫中穿行」——

但我不喜欢古巴平头百姓在街上讲的那种。他们的嗓音和语调单薄而急切，语速太快，唇音太多，并且总的说来给人以一种热烈与稚气相混合的印象；我的感觉是，这里的趋势是使这门语言衰弱下去，夺去它元音的开度和硬辅音的力度。这不过是几个钟头观察的批评，未必中肯；但久与此语相处的人那里，我也听到过同样的说法。据说在受过良好教育的古巴人中间，卡斯提利亚语的标准保持得很高，人们也颇有一番企及的雄心。

===Original===
After dinner, walked along the Paseo de Isabel Segunda, to see the pleasure-driving, which begins at about five o’clock, and lasts until dark. The most common carriage is the volante, but there are some carriages in the English style, with servants in livery on the box. I have taken a fancy for the strange looking two-horse volante. The postilion, the long, dangling traces, the superfluousness of a horse to be ridden by the man that guides the other, and the prodigality of silver, give the whole a look of style that eclipses the neat, appropriate English equipage. The ladies ride in full dress, *décolletées*, without hats. The servants on the carriages are not all Negroes. Many of the drivers are whites. The drives are along the Paseo de Isabel, across the Campo del Marte, and then along the Paseo de Tacon, a beautiful double avenue, lined with trees, which leads two or three miles, in a straight line, into the country.

===Chinese===
正餐后，沿伊莎贝尔二世林荫大道散步，看兜风的车马，这从五点钟左右开始，持续到天黑。最常见的车是沃兰特，但也有几辆英国式的马车，车夫座上坐着穿号衣的仆人。我迷上了那种模样古怪的双马沃兰特。骑辕的驭者、长长垂荡的挽索、多出一匹专供驭者骑坐来引导另一匹的马、挥霍的白银，使整体有一种气派，把整洁合度的英国车驾都比了下去。女士们盛装出游，袒胸露肩（*décolletées*），不戴帽子。车上的仆人不全是黑人，许多驭者是白人。车行沿伊莎贝尔林荫道，穿过战神广场，再沿塔康林荫大道走——那是一条美丽的双层林荫道，绿树夹峙，笔直地通向乡间，长两三英里。

===Original===
At 8 o’clock, drove to the Plaza de Armas, a square in front of the governor’s house, to hear the *Retreta*, at which a military band plays for an hour, every evening. There is a clear moon above, and a blue field of glittering stars; the air is pure and balmy; the band of fifty or sixty instruments discourses most eloquent music under the shade of palm trees and mangoes; the walks are filled with promenaders, and the streets around the square lined with carriages, in which the ladies recline, and receive the salutations and visits of the gentlemen. Very few ladies walk in the square, and those probably are strangers. It is against the etiquette for ladies to walk in public in Havana.

I walk leisurely home, in order to see Havana by night. The evening is the busiest season for the shops. Much of the business of shopping is done after gas lighting. Volantes and coaches are driving to and fro, and stopping at the shop doors, and attendants take their goods to the doors of the carriages. The watchmen stand at the corners of the streets, each carrying a long pike and a lantern. Billiard rooms and cafés are filled, and all who can walk for pleasure will walk now. This is also the principal time for paying visits.

===Chinese===
八点钟，驱车前往武器广场——总督府前的方场——去听晚乐会（*Retreta*），军乐队每晚在此演奏一小时。头顶是一轮皓月，一片缀满闪烁星斗的蓝天；空气纯净而和煦；五六十件乐器的乐队在棕榈树和芒果树的浓荫下奏出最雄辩的音乐；步道上满是散步的人，广场四周的街上停满了马车，女士们斜倚车中，接受绅士们的致意与拜会。广场上很少有女士步行，有也多半是外乡人。在哈瓦那，女士在公共场所步行是有违礼俗的。

我缓步走回住处，为的是看看夜间的哈瓦那。晚间是店铺最忙的时节。买东西的营生大半是在煤气灯点起之后做的。沃兰特和四轮马车来来往往，停在店门口，伙计把货物送到车门口。更夫站在街角，个个拿着长矛和灯笼。弹子房和咖啡馆座无虚席，凡是能出来走走的人这时候都要走走。这也是拜客的主要时辰。

===Original===
There is one strange custom observed here in all the houses. In the chief room, rows of chairs are placed, facing each other, three or four or five in each line, and always running at right angles with the street wall of the house. As you pass along the street, you look up this row of chairs. In these, the family and the visitors take their seats, in formal order. As the windows are open, deep, and large, with wide gratings and no glass, one has the inspection of the interior arrangement of all the front parlors of Havana, and can see what every lady wears, and who is visiting her. To bed early, after so exciting a day as one’s first day in the tropics.

===Chinese===
这里家家户户都守着一种奇特的习俗。正屋里摆着一排排椅子，相对而列，每排三四把或五把，而且总是与临街的墙面成直角。你沿街走过，正好顺着一排排椅子望进去。家人和来客就按正式的次序坐在这些椅子上。窗户敞开、又深又大，装着宽铁栅而没有玻璃，于是哈瓦那所有临街客厅的内部陈设尽收眼底，每位女士穿的什么、谁在拜访她，都看得一清二楚。在热带的第一天如此兴奋之后，早早安寝。

## VI Early morning in the city﻿—The chain gang﻿—Soldiers﻿—Baños de Mar﻿—The Cathedral﻿—Mass﻿—Tomb of Columbus / 六　城中清晨——锁链苦役队——士兵——海滨浴场——大教堂——弥撒——哥伦布之墓

===Original===
If mosquito nets were invented for the purpose of shutting mosquitoes in with you, they answer their purpose very well. The beds have no mattresses, and you lie on the hard sacking. This favors coolness and neatness. I should fear a mattress, in the economy of our hotel, at least. Where there is nothing but an iron frame, canvas stretched over it, and sheets and a blanket, you may know what you are dealing with.

The clocks of the churches and castles strike the quarter hours, and at each stroke the watchmen blow a kind of boatswain’s whistle, and cry the time and the state of the weather, which, from their name (*serenos*), should be always pleasant.

I have been advised to close the shutters at night, whatever the heat, as the change of air that often takes place before dawn is injurious; and I notice that many of the bedrooms in the hotel are closed, both doors and shutters, at night. This is too much for my endurance, and I venture to leave the air to its course, not being in the draught. One is also cautioned not to step with bare feet on the floor, for fear of the nigua (or *chigua*), a very small insect, that is said to enter the skin and build tiny nests, and lay little eggs that can only be seen by the microscope, but are tormenting and sometimes dangerous. This may be excessive caution, but it is so easy to observe, that it is not worthwhile to test the question.

===Chinese===
蚊帐若是为把蚊子关在里面陪你而发明的，那它们把自己的用途贯彻得很好。床没有褥子，人躺在硬绷绷的帆布床屉上。这倒成全了凉爽和洁净。至少，在我们这家旅馆的经济制度里，我是要怕一床褥子的。当这里只有铁架、绷在上面的帆布、被单和一条毯子时，你总知道自己面对的是什么。

教堂和城堡的钟敲着刻钟，每一响，更夫便吹一声水手长哨子似的哨音，报出时辰和天气；而从他们的名号（*serenos*，本义「晴朗」）来看，天气本该总是晴好的。

有人劝我夜里无论多热都要关上百叶窗，因为拂晓前常有变天，于身体有害；我还注意到旅馆里许多卧室夜里门和百叶窗都关得严严的。这超出了我的忍受力，我便斗胆听任空气自行其是，反正我不在风口上。还有人告诫不可赤脚踩地，为的是防沙蚤（nigua，或称 *chigua*）——一种极小的虫子，据说会钻进皮肤，筑起微小的巢，产下只有显微镜才看得见的小卵，却折磨人，有时还很危险。这也许是过分的谨慎，但遵守它太容易了，犯不着去验证这个疑问。

===Original===
*Saturday, February 19*.﻿—There are streaks of a clear dawn; it is nearly six o’clock, the cocks are crowing, and the drums and trumpets sounding. We have been told of sea baths, cut in the rock, near the Punta, at the foot of our Paseo. I walk down, under the trees, towards the Presidio. What is this clanking sound? Can it be cavalry, marching on foot, their sabres rattling on the pavement? No, it comes from that crowd of poor looking creatures that are forming in files in front of the Presidio. It is the chain gang! Poor wretches! I come nearer to them, and wait until they are formed and numbered and marched off. Each man has an iron band riveted round his ankle, and another round his waist, and the chain is fastened, one end into each of these bands, and dangles between them, clanking with every movement. This leaves the wearers free to use their arms, and, indeed, their whole body, it being only a weight and a badge and a note for discovery, from which they cannot rid themselves. It is kept on them day and night, working, eating, or sleeping. In some cases, two are chained together. They have passed their night in the Presidio (the great prison and garrison), and are marshalled for their day’s toil in the public streets and on the public works, in the heat of the sun. They look thoroughly wretched. Can any of these be political offenders? It is said that Carlists, from Old Spain, worked in this gang. Sentence to the chain gang in summer, in the case of a foreigner, must be nearly certain death.

===Chinese===
*2 月 19 日，星期六*。——天边透出清朗的曙色；将近六点，鸡在啼，鼓号在响。有人对我们说起过海边浴池，凿在岩石里，在蓬塔附近、我们那条林荫大道的尽头。我沿树荫往下走，朝要塞监狱方向去。这是什么叮当作响的声音？莫非是骑兵徒步行军，马刀在石路上碰响？不，声音来自那群正在要塞监狱前排队的衣衫寒碜的可怜人。是锁链苦役队！可怜的不幸者！我走近他们，等着他们列好队、报完数、被押走。每个人的脚踝上铆着一道铁箍，腰上又一道，锁链一端扣进这道箍、一端扣进那道箍，悬在中间，一动就哐啷作响。这样戴的人手臂乃至全身仍可自由活动；那不过是一副重量、一种印记、一张便于缉拿的条子，而他们自己摆脱不掉。日夜戴着它，做工、吃饭、睡觉都戴着。有些是两人锁在一起。他们在要塞监狱（大监狱兼驻防要塞）里过夜，此刻被编排起来，去公共街道和公共工程上顶着烈日服一天的苦役。他们看上去惨到十足。这些人里会不会有政治犯？据说旧西班牙来的卡洛斯派就在这支队伍里做过工。对外国人来说，夏天被判进锁链苦役队，几乎必死无疑。

===Original===
Farther on, between the Presidio and the Punta, the soldiers are drilling; and the drummers and trumpeters are practising on the rampart of the city walls.

A little to the left, in the Calzada de San Lázaro, are the Baños de Mar. These are boxes, each about twelve feet square and six or eight feet deep, cut directly into the rock which here forms the sea line, with steps of rock, and each box having a couple of portholes through which the waves of this tideless shore wash in and out. This arrangement is necessary, as sharks are so abundant that bathing in the open sea is dangerous. The pure rock, and the flow and reflow, make these bathing boxes very agreeable, and the water, which is that of the Gulf Stream, is at a temperature of 72°. The baths are roofed over, and partially screened on the in side, but open for a view out, on the side towards the sea; and as you bathe, you see the big ships floating up the Gulf Stream, that great highway of the Equinoctial world. The water stands at depths of from three to five feet in the baths; and they are large enough for short swimming. The bottom is white with sand and shells. These baths are made at the public expense, and are free. Some are marked for women, some for men, and some “*por la gente de color*.” A little further down the Calzada, is another set of baths, and further out in the suburbs, opposite the Beneficencia, are still others.
